- [ ] **Step 3: Write the job**

Create `packages/api/src/jobs/purgeShopActivity.ts`:

```ts
import type { TaskConfig } from "payload";

export const SHOP_ACTIVITY_RETENTION_MONTHS = 24;

/** Calendar months, not 730 days: "two years of history" is what a seller is told. */
function cutoff(now: Date): string {
	const at = new Date(now.getTime());
	at.setUTCMonth(at.getUTCMonth() - SHOP_ACTIVITY_RETENTION_MONTHS);
	return at.toISOString();
}

/**
 * Monthly rather than nightly: the log is append-only and bounded by member
 * activity, so a month of drift costs nothing, and a nightly scan of a
 * collection whose oldest row is normally inside the window is pure load.
 */
export const purgeShopActivityTask: TaskConfig<"purgeShopActivity"> = {
	slug: "purgeShopActivity",
	retries: 1,
	inputSchema: [],
	schedule: [{ cron: "0 4 1 * *", queue: "nightly" }],
	handler: async ({ req }) => {
		const stale = await req.payload.find({
			collection: "shop-activity-log",
			where: { createdAt: { less_than: cutoff(new Date()) } },
			depth: 0,
			limit: 1000,
			overrideAccess: true,
		});

		let deleted = 0;
		for (const entry of stale.docs) {
			await req.payload.delete({
				collection: "shop-activity-log",
				id: entry.id,
				overrideAccess: true,
				// `overrideAccess` is what gets past the collection's
				// `delete: () => false`; the context flag is not a gate, it marks
				// the write as the retention job's for anyone reading a log.
				context: { shopActivityService: true },
			});
			deleted += 1;
		}

		return { output: { deleted } };
	},
};
```

`packages/api/src/payload.config.ts`: add it to the `jobs.tasks` import and array. No new `autoRun` entry: `{ cron: "0 0 * * *", queue: "nightly", limit: 10 }` already ticks that queue, and the task's own `schedule` decides eligibility.

```ts
import { describe, expect, test } from "bun:test";
import {
	can,
	canSeeCost,
	isShopOwner,
	ROLE_PERMISSIONS,
	type ShopPermission,
} from "./shop-roles";

/** The same table as `packages/api/tests/int/shop-permissions.int.spec.ts`. */
const MATRIX: Array<[ShopPermission, boolean, boolean, boolean]> = [
	["catalogue.edit", true, true, true],
	["catalogue.archive", true, true, false],
	["stock.move", true, true, true],
	["costs.view", true, true, false],
	["costs.edit", true, true, false],
	["orders.view", true, true, true],
	["orders.process", true, true, true],
	["orders.cancel", true, true, false],
	["inbox.reply", true, true, true],
	["inbox.assignOthers", true, true, false],
	["payments.view", true, true, false],
	["payments.manage", true, false, false],
	["team.view", true, true, true],
	["team.inviteStaff", true, true, false],
	["team.manageManagers", true, false, false],
	["settings.edit", true, true, false],
	["settings.handle", true, false, false],
	["verification.submit", true, false, false],
	["resale.manage", true, true, false],
	["activity.view", true, true, false],
	["shop.close", true, false, false],
];

describe("can", () => {
	test("matches the server's matrix exactly", () => {
		for (const [permission, owner, manager, staff] of MATRIX) {
			expect([permission, can("owner", permission)]).toEqual([permission, owner]);
			expect([permission, can("manager", permission)]).toEqual([permission, manager]);
			expect([permission, can("staff", permission)]).toEqual([permission, staff]);
		}
	});

	test("grants nothing without a role", () => {
		expect(can(null, "team.view")).toBe(false);
		expect(can(undefined, "catalogue.edit")).toBe(false);
	});

	test("declares 21, 16 and 6 permissions", () => {
		expect(ROLE_PERMISSIONS.owner.length).toBe(21);
		expect(ROLE_PERMISSIONS.manager.length).toBe(16);
		expect(ROLE_PERMISSIONS.staff.length).toBe(6);
	});
});

describe("the two aliases the shipped screens use", () => {
	test("canSeeCost is costs.view", () => {
		expect(canSeeCost("manager")).toBe(true);
		expect(canSeeCost("staff")).toBe(false);
	});

	test("isShopOwner is still the role, not a permission", () => {
		expect(isShopOwner("owner")).toBe(true);
		expect(isShopOwner("manager")).toBe(false);
	});
});
```

		for (const key of [
			teamKey("s-1"),

			activityKey("s-1", { actor: "u-1" }),
			inboxRootKey("s-1"),

		}
	});

	test("a filtered activity or inbox key is covered by its own root", () => {
		expect(isKeyCoveredBy(activityKey("s-1", { actor: "u-1" }), activityRootKey("s-1"))).toBe(true);

	});

	});

	test("one shop's keys never match another shop's scope", () => {
		expect(isKeyCoveredBy(teamKey("s-1"), shopScopeKey("s-2"))).toBe(false);
	});
});

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the permission mirror**

Replace `packages/web/src/lib/shop-roles.ts`:

```ts
import type { ShopRole } from "~/types";

export type ShopPermission =
	| "catalogue.edit"
	| "catalogue.archive"
	| "stock.move"
	| "costs.view"
	| "costs.edit"
	| "orders.view"
	| "orders.process"
	| "orders.cancel"
	| "inbox.reply"
	| "inbox.assignOthers"
	| "payments.view"
	| "payments.manage"
	| "team.view"
	| "team.inviteStaff"
	| "team.manageManagers"
	| "settings.edit"
	| "settings.handle"
	| "verification.submit"
	| "resale.manage"
	| "activity.view"
	| "shop.close";

/**
 * Mirrors `ROLE_PERMISSIONS` in `packages/api/src/access/shopRoles.ts`, which
 * stays the authority. This copy exists so a screen can hide a control the
 * server would refuse rather than offering it and showing an error; the test
 * beside this file transcribes the same table the API's own test does, so a
 * divergence fails a test instead of shipping.
 */
export const ROLE_PERMISSIONS: Record<ShopRole, readonly ShopPermission[]> = {
	owner: [
		"catalogue.edit", "catalogue.archive", "stock.move",
		"costs.view", "costs.edit",
		"orders.view", "orders.process", "orders.cancel",
		"inbox.reply", "inbox.assignOthers",
		"payments.view", "payments.manage",
		"team.view", "team.inviteStaff", "team.manageManagers",
		"settings.edit", "settings.handle", "verification.submit",
		"resale.manage", "activity.view", "shop.close",
	],
	manager: [
		"catalogue.edit", "catalogue.archive", "stock.move",
		"costs.view", "costs.edit",
		"orders.view", "orders.process", "orders.cancel",
		"inbox.reply", "inbox.assignOthers",
		"payments.view",
		"team.view", "team.inviteStaff",
		"settings.edit",
		"resale.manage", "activity.view",
	],
	staff: [
		"catalogue.edit", "stock.move",
		"orders.view", "orders.process",
		"inbox.reply", "team.view",
	],
};

const SETS: Record<ShopRole, ReadonlySet<ShopPermission>> = {
	owner: new Set(ROLE_PERMISSIONS.owner),
	manager: new Set(ROLE_PERMISSIONS.manager),
	staff: new Set(ROLE_PERMISSIONS.staff),
};

export function can(
	role: ShopRole | null | undefined,
	permission: ShopPermission,
): boolean {
	if (!role) return false;
	return SETS[role]?.has(permission) ?? false;
}

/** Kept for the catalogue and stock screens that already call it. */
export function canSeeCost(role: ShopRole | null | undefined): boolean {
	return can(role, "costs.view");
}

/**
 * Still a role check, not a permission: the handle and closing are
 * `settings.handle` and `shop.close`, but several screens ask the plainer
 * question "is this person the owner".
 */
export function isShopOwner(role: ShopRole | null | undefined): boolean {
	return role === "owner";
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Add the query keys**

Append to `packages/web/src/lib/query-keys.ts`:

```ts
/** The roster and the pending invitations: one query, one key. */
export const teamKey = (shopId: string) =>
	[...shopScopeKey(shopId), "team"] as const;

/** Every filter of one shop's activity log, so a member action drops them all. */
export const activityRootKey = (shopId: string) =>
	[...shopScopeKey(shopId), "activity"] as const;

export const activityKey = (
	shopId: string,
	filters: { actor?: string; action?: string; targetType?: string } = {},
) => [...activityRootKey(shopId), filters] as const;

/** Every filter and page of one shop's inbox. */
export const inboxRootKey = (shopId: string) =>
	[...shopScopeKey(shopId), "inbox"] as const;

export const inboxKey = (
	shopId: string,
	filters: { filter?: string; q?: string } = {},
) => [...inboxRootKey(shopId), filters] as const;

/**
 * Outside `shopScopeKey` on purpose: it spans shops, so no single shop's
 * invalidation should drop it — the mutations that change membership
 * invalidate it by name.
 */
export const myShopsKey = () => ["me", "shops"] as const;

/** Also outside: the caller is not a member of the shop yet. */
export const invitationKey = (token: string) =>
	["invitations", token] as const;
```

- [ ] **Step 7: Add the transport and the hooks**

`packages/web/src/lib/shop-api.ts` — eighteen methods on the existing `shopApi` object, each a one-line `request` call. The complete set:

```ts
	listTeam: (shopId: string) => request<TeamView>(`/api/shops/${shopId}/members`),
	invite: (
		shopId: string,
		body: { channel: "phone" | "email"; phone?: string; email?: string; role: "manager" | "staff" },
	) =>
		request<{ invitation: PendingInvitationView; delivered: boolean }>(
			`/api/shops/${shopId}/invitations`,
			{ method: "POST", body: JSON.stringify(body) },
		),
	resendInvitation: (shopId: string, invitationId: string) =>
		request<{ invitation: PendingInvitationView; delivered: boolean }>(
			`/api/shops/${shopId}/invitations/${invitationId}/resend`,
			{ method: "POST" },
		),
	revokeInvitation: (shopId: string, invitationId: string) =>
		request<{ revoked: true }>(`/api/shops/${shopId}/invitations/${invitationId}`, {
			method: "DELETE",
		}),
	changeMemberRole: (shopId: string, memberId: string, role: "manager" | "staff") =>
		request<TeamMemberView>(`/api/shops/${shopId}/members/${memberId}`, {
			method: "PATCH",
			body: JSON.stringify({ role }),
		}),
	removeMember: (shopId: string, memberId: string) =>
		request<{ removed: true }>(`/api/shops/${shopId}/members/${memberId}`, {
			method: "DELETE",
		}),
	leaveShop: (shopId: string) =>
		request<{ left: true }>(`/api/shops/${shopId}/members/leave`, { method: "POST" }),
	updateInboxPreference: (shopId: string, inboxNotifications: InboxNotificationPreference) =>
		request<TeamMemberView>(`/api/shops/${shopId}/members/me`, {
			method: "PATCH",
			body: JSON.stringify({ inboxNotifications }),
		}),
	listActivity: (
		shopId: string,
		filters: { actor?: string; action?: string; targetType?: string; cursor?: string },
	) =>
		request<{ docs: ShopActivityView[]; nextCursor: string | null }>(
			`/api/shops/${shopId}/activity?${new URLSearchParams(
				Object.entries(filters).filter(([, value]) => Boolean(value)) as [string, string][],
			)}`,
		),
	listInbox: (shopId: string, filters: { filter?: InboxFilter; q?: string; cursor?: string }) =>
		request<InboxPage>(
			`/api/shops/${shopId}/inbox?${new URLSearchParams(
				Object.entries(filters).filter(([, value]) => Boolean(value)) as [string, string][],
			)}`,
		),
	assignConversation: (conversationId: string, userId: string | null) =>
		request<InboxConversationView>(`/api/conversations/${conversationId}/assign`, {
			method: "POST",
			body: JSON.stringify({ userId }),
		}),
	setConversationStatus: (conversationId: string, status: "open" | "done") =>
		request<InboxConversationView>(`/api/conversations/${conversationId}/status`, {
			method: "POST",
			body: JSON.stringify({ status }),
		}),
	markConversationRead: (conversationId: string, lastMessageId: string) =>
		request<{ lastReadAt: string; unreadCount: number }>(
			`/api/conversations/${conversationId}/read`,
			{ method: "POST", body: JSON.stringify({ lastMessageId }) },
		),
	startConversation: (listingId: string) =>
		request<{ conversationId: string; created: boolean }>(`/api/conversations/start`, {
			method: "POST",
			body: JSON.stringify({ listingId }),
		}),
	listMyShops: () => request<MyShopsEntry[]>(`/api/me/shops`),
	lookupInvitation: (token: string) =>
		request<PublicInvitationView>(`/api/public/invitations/${encodeURIComponent(token)}`),
	acceptInvitation: (token: string) =>
		request<{ shopId: string; role: ShopRole }>(
			`/api/invitations/${encodeURIComponent(token)}/accept`,
			{ method: "POST" },
		),
	declineInvitation: (token: string) =>
		request<{ declined: true }>(
			`/api/invitations/${encodeURIComponent(token)}/decline`,
			{ method: "POST" },
		),
```

`packages/web/src/hooks/use-shop-team.ts` — the query plus seven mutations, every one invalidating `teamKey(shopId)` and, where membership changed, `myShopsKey()`:

```ts
"use client";

import { useMutation, useQuery, useQueryClient } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { myShopsKey, teamKey } from "~/lib/query-keys";
import { shopApi } from "~/lib/shop-api";
import type { InboxNotificationPreference, TeamView } from "~/types";

export function useShopTeam(shopId: string | null) {
	return useQuery<TeamView>({
		queryKey: teamKey(shopId ?? ""),
		queryFn: () => shopApi.listTeam(shopId ?? ""),
		enabled: Boolean(shopId),
		retry: false,
	});
}

function useTeamMutation<TVars, TData>(
	shopId: string,
	mutationFn: (vars: TVars) => Promise<TData>,
	options: { alsoMyShops?: boolean } = {},
) {
	const queryClient = useQueryClient();
	return useMutation({
		mutationFn,
		onSuccess: () => {
			queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: teamKey(shopId) });
			if (options.alsoMyShops) {
				queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: myShopsKey() });
			}
		},
	});
}

export const useInviteMember = (shopId: string) =>
	useTeamMutation(shopId, (vars: Parameters<typeof shopApi.invite>[1]) =>
		shopApi.invite(shopId, vars),
	);

export const useResendInvitation = (shopId: string) =>
	useTeamMutation(shopId, (invitationId: string) =>
		shopApi.resendInvitation(shopId, invitationId),
	);

export const useRevokeInvitation = (shopId: string) =>
	useTeamMutation(shopId, (invitationId: string) =>
		shopApi.revokeInvitation(shopId, invitationId),
	);

export const useChangeMemberRole = (shopId: string) =>
	useTeamMutation(
		shopId,
		(vars: { memberId: string; role: "manager" | "staff" }) =>
			shopApi.changeMemberRole(shopId, vars.memberId, vars.role),
		{ alsoMyShops: true },
	);

export const useRemoveMember = (shopId: string) =>
	useTeamMutation(shopId, (memberId: string) => shopApi.removeMember(shopId, memberId), {
		alsoMyShops: true,
	});

export const useLeaveShop = (shopId: string) =>
	useTeamMutation(shopId, () => shopApi.leaveShop(shopId), { alsoMyShops: true });

export const useInboxPreference = (shopId: string) =>
	useTeamMutation(shopId, (preference: InboxNotificationPreference) =>
		shopApi.updateInboxPreference(shopId, preference),
	);
```

`use-shop-activity.ts`, `use-shop-inbox.ts`, `use-my-shops.ts` and `use-invitation.ts` follow the same shape:

- `useShopActivity(shopId, filters)` → `useInfiniteQuery` on `activityKey(shopId, filters)`, `getNextPageParam: (last) => last.nextCursor`.
- `useShopInbox(shopId, filters)` → `useInfiniteQuery` on `inboxKey(shopId, filters)`, `placeholderData: keepPreviousData` so the totals stay on screen while a filter changes.
- `useAssignConversation(shopId)` and `useConversationStatus(shopId)` invalidate `inboxRootKey(shopId)`; `useMarkConversationRead()` invalidates `inboxRootKey(shopId)` and `myShopsKey()` (the badge).
- `useMyShops()` → `useQuery` on `myShopsKey()`.
- `useInvitation(token)` → `useQuery` on `invitationKey(token)`, `retry: false`, `enabled: Boolean(token)`. `useAcceptInvitation(token)` and `useDeclineInvitation(token)` invalidate `invitationKey(token)` and `myShopsKey()`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the pure module**

Create `packages/web/src/lib/team-form.ts`:

```ts
import { z } from "zod";
import { can } from "~/lib/shop-roles";
import type { ShopRole, TeamMemberView, TeamView } from "~/types";

/**
 * The same shape `POST /api/shops/{id}/invitations` validates. The
 * conditional requirement is a `superRefine` rather than a discriminated
 * union so the form can keep both fields mounted while the segmented control
 * switches, without losing what the user already typed.
 */
export const inviteSchema = z
	.object({
		channel: z.enum(["phone", "email"]),
		phone: z.string().trim().optional(),
		email: z.string().trim().optional(),
		role: z.enum(["manager", "staff"]),
	})
	.superRefine((value, ctx) => {
		if (value.channel === "phone" && !value.phone) {
			ctx.addIssue({ code: "custom", path: ["phone"], message: "required" });
		}
		if (value.channel === "email") {
			if (!value.email) {
				ctx.addIssue({ code: "custom", path: ["email"], message: "required" });
				return;
			}
			if (!z.string().email().safeParse(value.email).success) {
				ctx.addIssue({ code: "custom", path: ["email"], message: "invalid" });
			}
		}
	});

export type InviteFormValues = z.infer<typeof inviteSchema>;

export function assignableRoles(
	role: ShopRole | null | undefined,
): Array<"manager" | "staff"> {
	const roles: Array<"manager" | "staff"> = [];
	if (can(role, "team.manageManagers")) roles.push("manager");
	if (can(role, "team.inviteStaff")) roles.push("staff");
	return roles;
}

/** Ownership transfer is out of scope, so the owner's row offers nothing. */
export function memberActions(
	callerRole: ShopRole | null | undefined,
	member: TeamMemberView,
): { canChangeRole: boolean; canRemove: boolean } {
	if (member.role === "owner") {
		return { canChangeRole: false, canRemove: false };
	}
	return {
		canChangeRole: can(callerRole, "team.manageManagers"),
		canRemove:
			member.role === "manager"
				? can(callerRole, "team.manageManagers")
				: can(callerRole, "team.inviteStaff"),
	};
}

/**
 * A pending invitation holds a seat, which is why the meter reads "5 / 5"
 * before the fifth person has accepted anything. `overage` is non-zero only
 * after a level drop shrinks the cap below the roster; nobody is removed,
 * and new invitations are refused until the count fits.
 */
export function seatSummary(team: TeamView): {
	used: number;
	total: number;
	overage: number;
} {
	const used = team.activeCount + team.invitations.length;
	return {
		used,
		total: team.maxMembers,
		overage: Math.max(0, team.activeCount - team.maxMembers),
	};
}
```

`packages/web/src/app/seller/team/page.tsx` — a Server Component that reads the caller's shop and role and hands them to the client:

```tsx
import { getTranslations } from "next-intl/server";
import { getMyShop } from "~/lib/server-shop";
import { TeamClient } from "./team-client";

export const metadata = { title: "Team" };

export default async function TeamPage() {
	const mine = await getMyShop();
	const t = await getTranslations("Team");
	if (!mine?.shop) return <p>{t("noShop")}</p>;
	return <TeamClient shopId={mine.shop.id} role={mine.role} />;
}
```

`team-client.tsx` (`"use client"`) holds one `useReducer` for the screen's related state and stays under 200 lines by delegating to the five components:

```tsx
type State = {
	inviteOpen: boolean;
	confirmRemove: TeamMemberView | null;
	roleTarget: TeamMemberView | null;
};

const initialState: State = { inviteOpen: false, confirmRemove: null, roleTarget: null };

function reducer(state: State, patch: Partial<State>): State {
	return { ...state, ...patch };
}
```

It renders, in order: `TeamLimitMeter`, the `Invite` button (only when `assignableRoles(role).length > 0`), `MemberTable`, `PendingInvitations`, and `InviteDialog`. When `team.data?.teamMembers === false` it renders `TeamLocked` instead of all of it.

`team-limit-meter.tsx` reads `seatSummary(team)` and renders `used / total` plus, when `overage > 0`, the overage line. `member-table.tsx` renders one row per member with the avatar, the name, a role chip, the joined date, a `suspended` chip when `member.suspended`, and an actions menu gated by `memberActions(role, member)`. `pending-invitations.tsx` renders the masked target, the role, the expiry, and Resend / Revoke buttons, disabling Resend when `sendCount >= 3`. `invite-dialog.tsx` is a `react-hook-form` + `zodResolver(inviteSchema)` form with a segmented Phone / Email control, the `+237`-defaulted phone input, a role select limited to `assignableRoles(role)`, and `setError` mapping from the mutation's `ApiError.code`:

```tsx

					form.setError(values.channel === "phone" ? "phone" : "email", {
						message: t(`errors.${code}`),

				toast.error(messageFor(error));

```

```ts
import { describe, expect, test } from "bun:test";
import { accountHint, inviteStep } from "./invite-flow";
import type { PublicInvitationView } from "~/types";

const invitation = (over: Partial<PublicInvitationView> = {}): PublicInvitationView => ({
	shop: { name: "Akwa", handle: "akwa", logoUrl: null, badge: "identity" },
	role: "staff",
	channel: "email",
	maskedTarget: "b\u2022\u2022\u2022@example.com",
	inviterFirstName: "Aicha",
	expiresAt: "2026-10-08T00:00:00.000Z",
	status: "pending",
	...over,
});

const viewer = (
	over: Partial<{ email: string | null; phone: string | null; phoneVerifiedAt: string | null }> = {},
) => ({ email: "bruno@example.com", phone: null, phoneVerifiedAt: null, ...over });

describe("inviteStep", () => {
	test("loading while there is neither an invitation nor an error", () => {
		expect(inviteStep({ invitation: null, error: null, viewer: null, joined: false })).toBe("loading");
	});

	test("invalid on an unknown token", () => {
		expect(
			inviteStep({ invitation: null, error: "team.invitationInvalid", viewer: null, joined: false }),
		).toBe("invalid");
	});

	test("expired and responded read differently, because the answer differs", () => {
		expect(
			inviteStep({ invitation: invitation({ status: "expired" }), error: null, viewer: viewer(), joined: false }),
		).toBe("expired");
		for (const status of ["accepted", "declined", "revoked"] as const) {
			expect(
				inviteStep({ invitation: invitation({ status }), error: null, viewer: viewer(), joined: false }),
			).toBe("responded");
		}
	});

	test("asks a signed-out visitor to sign in", () => {
		expect(inviteStep({ invitation: invitation(), error: null, viewer: null, joined: false })).toBe("signIn");
	});

	test("is ready when the signed-in address is on the invited domain, case-insensitively", () => {
		expect(
			inviteStep({ invitation: invitation(), error: null, viewer: viewer({ email: "Bruno@Example.com" }), joined: false }),
		).toBe("ready");
	});

	test("is a mismatch when the signed-in address is on another domain", () => {
		expect(
			inviteStep({ invitation: invitation(), error: null, viewer: viewer({ email: "other@gmail.com" }), joined: false }),
		).toBe("mismatch");
	});

	test("cannot tell two accounts on the same domain apart, and lets the server decide", () => {
		// The local part is masked on purpose, so the page offers the action and
		// `team.invitationMismatch` from the accept is what corrects it.
		expect(
			inviteStep({ invitation: invitation(), error: null, viewer: viewer({ email: "someone@example.com" }), joined: false }),
		).toBe("ready");
	});

	test("asks for phone verification on a phone invitation with no verified number", () => {
		expect(
			inviteStep({
				invitation: invitation({ channel: "phone", maskedTarget: "+237 6\u2022\u2022 \u2022\u2022 \u20224 21" }),
				error: null,
				viewer: viewer({ phone: null, phoneVerifiedAt: null }),
				joined: false,
			}),
		).toBe("verifyPhone");
	});

	test("is ready on a phone invitation once a number is verified: the server decides the match", () => {
		expect(
			inviteStep({
				invitation: invitation({ channel: "phone" }),
				error: null,
				viewer: viewer({ phone: "+237612345421", phoneVerifiedAt: "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z" }),
				joined: false,
			}),
		).toBe("ready");
	});

	test("shows the mismatch the server reported", () => {
		expect(
			inviteStep({
				invitation: invitation({ channel: "phone" }),
				error: "team.invitationMismatch",
				viewer: viewer({ phone: "+237699999999", phoneVerifiedAt: "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z" }),
				joined: false,
			}),
		).toBe("mismatch");
	});

	test("joined wins over everything once the accept has landed", () => {
		expect(
			inviteStep({ invitation: invitation({ status: "accepted" }), error: null, viewer: viewer(), joined: true }),
		).toBe("joined");
	});
});

describe("accountHint", () => {
	test("hands the copy the channel and the masked target, and nothing more", () => {
		expect(accountHint(invitation())).toEqual({
			channel: "email",
			maskedTarget: "b\u2022\u2022\u2022@example.com",
		});
		expect(accountHint(invitation({ channel: "phone", maskedTarget: "+237 6\u2022\u2022 \u2022\u2022 \u20224 21" }))).toEqual({
			channel: "phone",
			maskedTarget: "+237 6\u2022\u2022 \u2022\u2022 \u20224 21",
		});
	});
});
```

There is deliberately no `registerPrefill`: the invited address is masked, so the register form cannot be prefilled from it. `accountHint` is what the sign-in and register copy shows instead — "create an account with the address this invitation was sent to, `b•••@example.com`".

Expected: FAIL — the module does not exist.

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the flow module**

```ts
import type { PublicInvitationView } from "~/types";

export type InviteStep =
	| "loading"
	| "invalid"
	| "expired"
	| "responded"
	| "signIn"
	| "verifyPhone"
	| "mismatch"
	| "ready"
	| "joined";

export interface InviteViewer {
	email: string | null;
	phone: string | null;
	phoneVerifiedAt: string | null;
}

/**
 * One function decides what the invite page shows, so the page itself has no
 * branching worth a component test. The server is the authority on whether a
 * target matches: this only decides which screen to offer, and `mismatch`
 * appears either because the domain plainly differs or because the server said
 * so on the accept.
 */
export function inviteStep(input: {
	invitation: PublicInvitationView | null;
	error: string | null;
	viewer: InviteViewer | null;
	joined: boolean;
}): InviteStep {
	if (input.joined) return "joined";
	if (input.error === "team.invitationMismatch") return "mismatch";
	if (input.error) return "invalid";
	if (!input.invitation) return "loading";
	if (input.invitation.status === "expired") return "expired";
	if (input.invitation.status !== "pending") return "responded";
	if (!input.viewer) return "signIn";

	if (input.invitation.channel === "phone") {
		// A verified number is the proof; whether it is the *right* number is
		// the server's call, not something a masked target can answer.
		return input.viewer.phoneVerifiedAt ? "ready" : "verifyPhone";
	}

	// Only the domain survives masking, so a domain mismatch is certain and a
	// domain match is not. Offering the action on a match and letting the
	// accept refuse is better than refusing someone who is in fact the invitee.
	const invitedDomain = (input.invitation.maskedTarget.split("@")[1] ?? "").toLowerCase();
	const mine = (input.viewer.email ?? "").toLowerCase();
	return invitedDomain !== "" && mine.endsWith(`@${invitedDomain}`) ? "ready" : "mismatch";
}

/** What the sign-in and register copy shows; never a prefillable value. */
export function accountHint(invitation: PublicInvitationView): {
	channel: "phone" | "email";
	maskedTarget: string;
} {
	return { channel: invitation.channel, maskedTarget: invitation.maskedTarget };
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Build the page**

`page.tsx` is a Server Component with `export const metadata = { robots: { index: false, follow: false } }` and renders `<InviteClient token={token} />`. `invite-client.tsx` (`"use client"`) renders the shop summary — logo, name, handle, `LevelBadge` from `invitation.shop.badge`, the role and its one-line description, the inviter's first name, the masked target, the expiry — then one of nine states from `inviteStep`:

- `signIn` → "Sign in to join {shop}" linking to `/auth/login?returnTo=/invite/{token}`, and "Create an account to join" linking to `/auth/register?returnTo=/invite/{token}`.
- `verifyPhone` → the existing phone-verification flow with the number field empty and the masked target shown as the hint.
- `mismatch` → the masked target and a "Switch account" link to `/auth/login?returnTo=/invite/{token}`.
- `ready` → "Join {shop}" (accept) and "Decline".
- `joined` → a success panel linking to `/seller`.
- `invalid`, `expired`, `responded` → the corresponding message and a link home.

Accept on success pushes `/seller` and calls `router.refresh()` so the seller layout re-reads the caller's shops.

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the module**

```ts
import type { ShopActivityAction, ShopActivityView } from "~/types";

/** The API's own list and order, so the filter select matches the enum. */
export const ACTIVITY_ACTIONS: readonly ShopActivityAction[] = [
	"member.invited",
	"member.invitation_resent",
	"member.invitation_revoked",
	"member.joined",
	"member.role_changed",
	"member.removed",
	"member.left",
	"member.paused",
	"member.resumed",
	"product.created",
	"product.updated",
	"product.published",
	"product.archived",
	"variant.price_changed",
	"variant.cost_changed",
	"stock.moved",
	"listing.attached",
	"listing.detached",
	"shop.updated",
	"shop.handle_changed",
	"shop.closed",
	"conversation.assigned",
	"conversation.status_changed",
	"verification.submitted",
];

export function activityLabelKey(action: ShopActivityAction): string {
	return `shopActivity.actions.${action}`;
}

export function activityTargetHref(entry: ShopActivityView): string | null {
	switch (entry.targetType) {
		case "product":
		case "variant":
			// A variant entry carries its product's id: a variant has no page.
			return `/seller/catalogue/${entry.targetId}`;
		case "listing":
			return `/listing/${entry.targetId}`;
		case "conversation":
			return `/seller/messages?conversation=${entry.targetId}`;
		case "verification-request":
			return "/seller/verification";
		default:
			// A member, an invitation and the shop have no page of their own that
			// a link here would usefully open.
			return null;
	}
}

const DASH = "—";

const show = (value: unknown): string => {
	if (value === null || value === undefined || value === "") return DASH;
	if (typeof value === "boolean") return value ? "true" : "false";
	if (typeof value === "number" || typeof value === "string") return String(value);
	return DASH;
};

/**
 * `metadata` is free-form json by design — each action records what it
 * changed. Only the `{ before, after }` shape is rendered as a diff; anything
 * else (a `cause`, a `sendCount`) is shown raw by the details drawer.
 */
export function activityChanges(
	entry: ShopActivityView,
): Array<{ field: string; before: string; after: string }> {
	const metadata = entry.metadata;
	if (!metadata || typeof metadata !== "object") return [];
	const before = (metadata as { before?: unknown }).before;
	const after = (metadata as { after?: unknown }).after;
	if (!before || typeof before !== "object" || Array.isArray(before)) return [];
	if (!after || typeof after !== "object" || Array.isArray(after)) return [];

	const fields = [
		...new Set([
			...Object.keys(before as Record<string, unknown>),
			...Object.keys(after as Record<string, unknown>),
		]),
	];
	return fields.map((field) => ({
		field,
		before: show((before as Record<string, unknown>)[field]),
		after: show((after as Record<string, unknown>)[field]),
	}));
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Build the screen**

`page.tsx` reads the shop and role, renders nothing but a `403`-style panel when `!can(role, "activity.view")`, and otherwise renders `<ActivityClient shopId={...} />`. `activity-client.tsx` holds one `useReducer` (`{ actor, action, targetType, openEntry }`), renders `ActivityFilters` (a member select from `useShopTeam`, an action select from `ACTIVITY_ACTIONS` with translated labels, a target-type select) and `ActivityTable` (when, who with their `actorRole`, the translated action, the target as a link from `activityTargetHref` or plain text, and a "Details" button opening `ActivityDetails`). The table pages with the infinite query's `fetchNextPage` behind a "Load more" button — 50 rows a page, as the server returns.

`activity-details.tsx` renders `activityChanges(entry)` as a two-column before/after list, and the remaining metadata keys as a plain definition list.

```ts
import { can } from "~/lib/shop-roles";
import type { MyShopsEntry, ShopRole, TeamMemberView, TeamView } from "~/types";

/** Read by the seller layout on the server and written by the switcher. */
export const ACTIVE_SHOP_COOKIE = "bns_active_shop";

/**
 * A cookie can name a shop the caller has since left or been removed from, so
 * it is a preference, never an authority: the list comes from
 * `GET /api/me/shops`, and anything not in it is ignored.
 */
export function resolveActiveShop(
	shops: MyShopsEntry[],
	cookieValue: string | null,
): MyShopsEntry | null {
	if (shops.length === 0) return null;
	const preferred = cookieValue
		? shops.find((entry) => entry.shopId === cookieValue)
		: undefined;
	return preferred ?? shops[0];
}

/**
 * Who the assignee picker offers. A staff member holds `inbox.reply` but not
 * `inbox.assignOthers`, so their list is themselves — which the server
 * enforces too; this only stops the UI offering a control that would fail.
 * A suspended member is left out: `inboxMemberIds` would refuse them.
 */
export function assignableMembers(
	team: TeamView | undefined,
	callerRole: ShopRole | null | undefined,
	callerId: string,
): TeamMemberView[] {
	const members = (team?.members ?? []).filter((member) => !member.suspended);
	if (can(callerRole, "inbox.assignOthers")) return members;
	return members.filter((member) => member.userId === callerId);
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the socket hook**

```ts
"use client";

import { useQueryClient } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { useRouter } from "next/navigation";
import { useTranslations } from "next-intl";
import { useEffect } from "react";
import { toast } from "sonner";
import { useChatClient } from "~/hooks/use-chat-client";
import { inboxRootKey, myShopsKey } from "~/lib/query-keys";

/**
 * A subscription, which is what `useEffect` is for. It never loads data: every
 * event invalidates a query and lets TanStack Query do the fetching.
 */
export function useShopInboxSocket(shopId: string | null): void {
	const { chatClient } = useChatClient();
	const queryClient = useQueryClient();
	const router = useRouter();
	const t = useTranslations("Inbox");

	useEffect(() => {
		if (!chatClient || !shopId) return;

		void chatClient.joinShopInbox(shopId);

		const onUpdated = () => {
			queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: inboxRootKey(shopId) });
			queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: myShopsKey() });
		};
		const onRevoked = (payload: { shopId: string }) => {
			if (payload.shopId !== shopId) return;
			// The socket has already been made to leave the rooms server-side;
			// this is the part the person sees.
			toast.error(t("accessRevoked"));
			router.push("/seller");
			router.refresh();
		};

		chatClient.on("inbox:conversation-updated", onUpdated);
		chatClient.on("message:new", onUpdated);
		chatClient.on("shop:access-revoked", onRevoked);

		return () => {
			chatClient.off("inbox:conversation-updated", onUpdated);
			chatClient.off("message:new", onUpdated);
			chatClient.off("shop:access-revoked", onRevoked);
			chatClient.leaveShopInbox(shopId);
		};
	}, [chatClient, shopId, queryClient, router, t]);
}
```

Web has no `useChatClient` yet — `messages-client.tsx` builds its own `ChatClient`. Extract the provider in this task: create `packages/web/src/hooks/use-chat-client.tsx` with a `ChatProvider` mirroring mobile's `ChatContext`, mount it in `packages/web/src/app/layout.tsx` inside the TanStack Query provider, and move `messages-client.tsx` onto it in the same commit. Two `ChatClient` instances on one page would open two sockets and double every event.

- [ ] **Step 4: Build the inbox screen**

`page.tsx` (Server Component) reads `cookies().get(ACTIVE_SHOP_COOKIE)`, calls `GET /api/me/shops` server-side, resolves the active shop with `resolveActiveShop`, and renders `<InboxClient shopId={...} role={...} viewerId={...} />`; with no shop it redirects to `/shop/new`.

`inbox-client.tsx` (`"use client"`, under 200 lines) holds

```tsx
type State = { filter: InboxFilter; q: string; selectedId: string | null };
```

in a `useReducer`, calls `useShopInboxSocket(shopId)`, and lays out three columns: `FilterList` (the six filters with their totals from `page.totals`), `ConversationList` (buyer, listing thumbnail, preview, relative time, assignee avatar, unread dot, and a "Load more" button from the infinite query), and `ThreadPanel`.

`ThreadPanel` renders the header — buyer name, a link to the listing, `AssigneePicker`, and "Mark done" / "Reopen" — plus the message list and a composer. It reads the thread through the existing `/api/messages?where[conversation][equals]=` query the personal messages screen already uses, sends through the chat client, and calls `useMarkConversationRead()` with the newest message id when the panel mounts or the conversation changes.

`AssigneePicker` offers `assignableMembers(team, role, viewerId)` plus "Unassign", shows a `suspended` chip on the current assignee when `conversation.assignee?.suspended`, and calls `useAssignConversation(shopId)`. Staff see only "Assign to me" and "Unassign".

`shop-switcher.tsx` renders a dropdown of `useMyShops().data`, shown only when there is more than one, writes `ACTIVE_SHOP_COOKIE` with `document.cookie` (path `/`, `SameSite=Lax`, one year) and calls `router.refresh()`. It goes into `seller-shell.tsx`'s header.

`packages/web/src/components/seller/seller-sidebar.tsx` — the `ITEMS` array gains a `permission` per entry and the component takes the caller's `role`:

```tsx
const ITEMS = [
	{ href: "/seller", key: "dashboard", icon: LayoutDashboard, exact: true, permission: null },
	{ href: "/seller/catalogue", key: "catalogue", icon: Package, exact: false, permission: "catalogue.edit" },
	{ href: "/seller/stock", key: "stock", icon: Boxes, exact: false, permission: "stock.move" },
	{ href: "/seller/messages", key: "inbox", icon: Inbox, exact: false, permission: "inbox.reply" },
	{ href: "/seller/team", key: "team", icon: Users, exact: false, permission: "team.view" },
	{ href: "/seller/team/activity", key: "activity", icon: History, exact: false, permission: "activity.view" },
	{ href: "/seller/verification", key: "verification", icon: BadgeCheck, exact: false, permission: "verification.submit" },
	{ href: "/messages", key: "messages", icon: MessageCircle, exact: false, permission: null },
	{ href: "/shop/manage", key: "settings", icon: Settings, exact: false, permission: "settings.edit" },
] as const satisfies ReadonlyArray<{
	href: string;
	key: string;
	icon: LucideIcon;
	exact: boolean;
	permission: ShopPermission | null;
}>;
```

and the render filters on `item.permission === null || can(role, item.permission)`. `seller-shell.tsx` passes `mine.role` down. The inbox entry carries an unread badge from `useMyShops()`'s `inboxUnread` for the active shop, the way `lowStock` already works.

This is where the spec's "staff do not see Payments, Verification, Settings or Activity" lands: Payments is P5 and not in the list yet, and the other three are now permission-gated.

	test("and still reads as the shop to the buyer", () => {

`secondary: "formerMember"` is a translation key, not copy; the component resolves it through `next-intl`. Note that in the module's comment so nobody renders it raw.

- [ ] **Step 2: Run it, then write the module**

Run: `cd packages/web && bun test src/lib/conversation-view.test.ts`
Expected: FAIL.

```ts
export interface ConversationHeader {
	title: string;
	subtitle: string | null;
	logoUrl: string | null;
	isShop: boolean;
}

/**
 * Who a conversation is "with" depends on which side is looking. A buyer is
 * talking to the shop, whoever answers; a member is talking to the buyer,
 * with the shop as context.
 */
export function conversationHeader(input: {
	shop: { name: string; logoUrl: string | null } | null;
	other: { name: string | null; avatarUrl: string | null } | null;
	viewerIsShopSide: boolean;
	buyerName: string | null;
}): ConversationHeader {
	if (input.shop && !input.viewerIsShopSide) {
		return {
			title: input.shop.name,
			subtitle: null,
			logoUrl: input.shop.logoUrl,
			isShop: true,
		};
	}
	if (input.shop && input.viewerIsShopSide) {
		return {
			title: input.other?.name ?? input.buyerName ?? "",
			subtitle: input.shop.name,
			logoUrl: input.other?.avatarUrl ?? null,
			isShop: false,
		};
	}
	return {
		title: input.other?.name ?? "",
		subtitle: null,
		logoUrl: input.other?.avatarUrl ?? null,
		isShop: false,
	};
}

/**
 * `secondary` is a translation key when it is `"formerMember"`, and a name
 * otherwise. The caller resolves it through next-intl rather than rendering it
 * raw — a buyer must never learn which member typed, which is why the shop
 * side collapses to the shop name for them.
 */
export function messageAuthorLabel(input: {
	senderSide: "buyer" | "shop" | null;
	shopName: string | null;
	senderFirstName: string | null;
	formerMemberAuthor: boolean;
	viewerIsShopSide: boolean;
}): { primary: string; secondary: string | null } {
	if (input.senderSide !== "shop") {
		return { primary: input.senderFirstName ?? "", secondary: null };
	}
	const primary = input.shopName ?? "";
	if (!input.viewerIsShopSide) return { primary, secondary: null };
	return {
		primary,
		secondary: input.formerMemberAuthor
			? "formerMember"
			: (input.senderFirstName ?? null),
	};
}
```

## Mobile (Tasks 25–30)

**How to port a web module to mobile, exactly.** These modules are pure and identical by design; retyping them is how two copies drift. Copy the file and rewrite only the import specifiers and the test runner, with the commands given in the step — never by hand. The two tests then assert the same behaviour in both packages, so a change to one that is not made to the other fails a test.

`app/seller/team/index.tsx` — `SellerHeader`, `TeamLimitMeter`, a `FlashList` of `MemberRow` (avatar, name, role chip, joined date, a `suspended` chip), then a `FlashList` of `PendingInvitationRow` (masked target, role, expiry, Resend and Revoke, with Resend disabled at `sendCount >= 3`), then an "Invite" button routing to `/seller/team/invite` when `assignableRoles(role).length > 0`. - `ready` → "Join {shop}" and "Decline".
- `joined` → routes to `/seller` and invalidates `shopKeys.myShops`.
- `invalid`, `expired`, `responded` → the message and a "Back home" action.

import { describe, expect, test } from "bun:test";
import { visibleSellerTiles } from "./sellerTiles";

const counts = { inboxUnread: 4, lowStock: 2 };

describe("visibleSellerTiles", () => {
	test("gives an owner every tile", () => {
		expect(visibleSellerTiles("owner", counts).map((t) => t.key)).toEqual([
			"catalogue",
			"stock",
			"inbox",
			"team",
			"activity",

	});

			"team",

	});

	test("omits a zero badge rather than rendering a zero", () => {

	});
});

Create `packages/mobile/src/locales/parity.test.ts`:

```ts
import { describe, expect, test } from "bun:test";
import en from "./en.json";
import fr from "./fr.json";

type Json = { [key: string]: Json | string };

function leafPaths(node: Json, prefix = ""): string[] {
	return Object.entries(node).flatMap(([key, value]) => {
		const path = prefix ? `${prefix}.${key}` : key;
		return typeof value === "string" ? [path] : leafPaths(value, path);
	});
}

/**
 * `apiError.locales.test.ts` guards the error codes. This guards everything
 * else: a string added to one language and forgotten in the other renders its
 * raw key path to half the users, which is how `filters.verified` sat
 * untranslated for a phase.
 */
describe("locale parity", () => {
	test("en and fr expose exactly the same keys", () => {
		const enKeys = leafPaths(en as Json).sort();
		const frKeys = leafPaths(fr as Json).sort();
		const onlyEn = enKeys.filter((key) => !frKeys.includes(key));
		const onlyFr = frKeys.filter((key) => !enKeys.includes(key));
		expect({ onlyEn, onlyFr }).toEqual({ onlyEn: [], onlyFr: [] });
	});

	test("every P3 namespace exists in both", () => {
		for (const namespace of ["team", "invite", "inbox", "shopActivity"]) {
			expect(Object.keys(en as Json)).toContain(namespace);
			expect(Object.keys(fr as Json)).toContain(namespace);
		}
	});

	test("every one of the twenty-four activity actions is translated in both", () => {
		const actions = (node: Json) =>
			Object.keys(((node.shopActivity as Json).actions as Json) ?? {});
		expect(actions(en as Json)).toHaveLength(24);
		expect(actions(fr as Json).sort()).toEqual(actions(en as Json).sort());
	});

	test("no string is left identical in both languages by accident in the P3 namespaces", () => {
		// Proper nouns and format strings legitimately match; a whole namespace
		// matching means one language was pasted over the other.
		for (const namespace of ["team", "invite", "inbox"]) {
			const enLeaves = leafPaths((en as Json)[namespace] as Json);
			const identical = enLeaves.filter((path) => {
				const read = (node: Json) =>
					path.split(".").reduce<Json | string | undefined>(
						(acc, key) => (acc && typeof acc === "object" ? acc[key] : undefined),
						node,
					);
				return read((en as Json)[namespace] as Json) === read((fr as Json)[namespace] as Json);
			});
			expect(identical.length).toBeLessThan(enLeaves.length);
		}
	});
});
```

`sellerTiles.ts`:

```ts
import { can, type ShopPermission } from "./shopRoles";
import type { ShopRole } from "../types/api";

export interface SellerTile {
	key: string;
	href: string;
	permission: ShopPermission | null;
	badge?: number;
}

const TILES: ReadonlyArray<{ key: string; href: string; permission: ShopPermission }> = [
	{ key: "catalogue", href: "/seller/catalogue", permission: "catalogue.edit" },
	{ key: "stock", href: "/seller/stock-adjust", permission: "stock.move" },
	{ key: "inbox", href: "/seller/inbox", permission: "inbox.reply" },
	{ key: "team", href: "/seller/team", permission: "team.view" },
	{ key: "activity", href: "/seller/activity", permission: "activity.view" },
	{ key: "settings", href: "/shop/manage", permission: "settings.edit" },
	{ key: "verification", href: "/seller/verification", permission: "verification.submit" },
];

/** A zero badge is omitted, not rendered: "0" beside a tile reads as broken. */
export function visibleSellerTiles(
	role: ShopRole | null | undefined,
	counts: { inboxUnread: number; lowStock: number },
): SellerTile[] {
	return TILES.filter((tile) => can(role, tile.permission)).map((tile) => {
		const badge =
			tile.key === "inbox"
				? counts.inboxUnread
				: tile.key === "stock"
					? counts.lowStock
					: 0;
		return { ...tile, ...(badge > 0 ? { badge } : {}) };
	});
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Change the four screens**

`app/seller/index.tsx` — replace the hand-written `manageTiles` array with `visibleSellerTiles(mine.role, { inboxUnread, lowStock })`, and add `ShopSwitcher` to the header when `useActiveShop().shops.length > 1`. The existing verification tile body stays: look it up by `key === "verification"` and keep `verificationTileBody`.

`app/(tabs)/messages/index.tsx` — add `where[participants][equals]=me` to the list query, and a "Shop" chip on a conversation whose `shop` is set.

`app/messages/[conversationId].tsx` — call `useMarkConversationRead()` on focus instead of the per-message read path, and render the header and the author labels through `conversationHeader` and `messageAuthorLabel`.

`app/listing/[id].tsx` — the "Message" action calls `useStartConversation()` and routes to `/messages/{conversationId}`.

- [ ] **Step 5: Close the parity gaps the tests named**

Run the two parity tests and add every key they list to the language that is missing it. The tests are the specification here: a key in one file and not the other is a failure, not a warning.

- [ ] **Step 6: Run every client check**

```bash
cd packages/mobile && bun test && bunx tsc --noEmit
cd packages/web && bun test && bun run check-types
```
Run: `bunx biome check --write packages/mobile/app packages/mobile/src packages/web/src/lib/messages-parity.test.ts`

- [ ] **Step 7: Commit**

## Release

### Task 31: Staging pass, then production

**Files:** none — this task is the release gate.

- [ ] **Step 1: Deploy to staging with two chat-service replicas**

Confirm before anything else: `shops.enabled` and `verification.enabled` are on in `AppSettings`, `REDIS_URL` reaches the same Redis from the API and both chat-service replicas, `CHAT_SERVICE_EMAIL` and `CHAT_SERVICE_PASSWORD` are set on chat-service and `CHAT_SERVICE_EMAIL` on the API, and the two P3 migrations have run. Two replicas are the point: cross-node eviction cannot be observed on one.

Then run `bun run sync:notifications` (or the script's own entry point) and confirm Novu lists the eight new workflows.

- [ ] **Step 2: The level-2 team pass**

With a level-2 staging shop:

- invite a staff member by phone to a number with **no** account: the SMS arrives bilingual, the link opens the web page, register → verify the phone → accept → the seller space of that shop opens;
- invite a manager by email to an **existing** account: the email arrives, the in-app notification arrives, accepting from the signed-in account works;
- invite the same email again: `team.invitationPending`;
- invite the owner's own number: `team.cannotInviteSelf`;
- a manager tries to invite a manager: refused; invites a staff member: allowed;
- a staff member opens `/seller/team`: the roster renders, no Invite button, no actions menu;
- fill the shop to five seats and invite again: `team.limitReached`, and the meter reads "5 / 5";
- resend an invitation, then open the **first** link: `team.invitationInvalid`; the second link works;
- resend twice more: the third resend is refused with `team.resendLimit`.

- [ ] **Step 3: The permission pass**

As a staff member, on web and on mobile:

- no cost, margin, stock value or stock summary anywhere;
- no Verification, Settings or Activity in the sidebar or among the tiles;
- archiving a product is not offered, and `PATCH` on it through the API answers `shop.forbidden`;
- `POST /api/shops/{id}/handle` answers `shop.forbidden`.

- [ ] **Step 4: The inbox pass**

- a buyer messages the shop from a listing: every member with `all` gets a push, the conversation appears in the shared inbox on both clients with the buyer, the listing and the preview;
- a second buyer message inside two minutes sends no second push;
- assign the conversation to a staff member: only they are notified from then on, and they get `shop-conversation-assigned`;
- the staff member replies: the buyer sees the **shop name**, and the buyer's thread header shows the shop logo; the member's first name appears only inside the inbox;
- mark done, then a new buyer message: it reopens;
- open the same conversation on web and on mobile at once and assign from each: one winner, and both screens settle on the same assignee;
- the buyer messages about a **second** listing of the same shop: the same thread, not a new one.

- [ ] **Step 5: The eviction pass — the one that needs two replicas**

- sign the staff member in on a device that lands on replica A (check the logs), and open the shop inbox;
- remove them from the owner's session, which lands on replica B;
- confirm on the device: the screen closes with the alert, and a send attempt on a shop conversation is refused;
- confirm in replica A's logs that the socket left the rooms — the event arrived through Redis, not from its own process;
- confirm `GET /api/shops/{id}/inbox` for that user answers `shop.notMember`.

Then the same for the two no-id cases:

- suspend the shop from moderation: every member's inbox screen closes, and the buyer and the shop both get `shop.inactive` on a send;
- revoke the shop's level 2 in P2: pending invitations go, `shop-team-paused` arrives for the owner and the members, the members' access goes, the owner keeps theirs, and the team page shows the locked state. Restore the level and confirm access comes back with no re-invitation.

- [ ] **Step 6: The moderation and deletion pass**

- suspend a member from mobile moderation: their inbox access goes, their assignment stays with a `suspended` chip on the assignee, the owner reassigns; lift the suspension and confirm access returns with no write;
- delete a member's account: their shop messages stay, attributed to the shop with "Former member", and their membership reads revoked;
- close the shop: every non-owner membership and every pending invitation reads revoked, and the activity log holds one `member.removed` per member plus `shop.closed`.

- [ ] **Step 7: The i18n pass**

Switch both clients to French and walk the team screen, the invite dialog, the invite page, the inbox with all six filters, and the activity list with every action visible. No raw key path anywhere. Then the same in English.

- [ ] **Step 8: Production**

Run both migrations, confirm `CHAT_SERVICE_EMAIL` is set on the API (without it `isChatServiceAccount` matches nobody and websocket messages stop persisting), sync the Novu workflows, deploy, and repeat Steps 2, 4 and 5 abbreviated against one real shop before announcing the feature.

- [ ] **Step 9: Commit the release note**

```bash
git add docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-15-p3-team.md
git commit -m "docs(plans): record the P3 team staging and production pass"
```

### Task 32: The moderator's Team section on the shop sheet

**Files:**
- Modify: `packages/api/src/app/(frontend)/api/moderation/shops/[id]/route.ts`
- Modify: `packages/mobile/src/components/moderation/ModerationScreen.tsx`
- Create: `packages/mobile/src/components/moderation/ShopTeamCard.tsx`
- Test: `packages/api/tests/int/moderation-shop-team.int.spec.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `recentShopActivity(payload, shopId, limit, req?)` from Task 3, and `listShopMembers(payload, shopId, req?)` from Task 9.
- Produces: nothing later tasks depend on. This is the last backend-to-mobile surface.

**Why this task exists, and why it is last.** The spec asks for a "Team" section on the P1 moderation shop sheet — members, roles, joined dates, and the last 20 activity entries. The plan's author could not name a file for it, because the spec describes the surface in a clause rather than naming it, and left it out with a note. Ruling: the surface is `GET /api/moderation/shops/[id]`, which already exists and which mobile's `ModerationScreen` already consumes. **Web has no moderation shop sheet at all** — only `/moderation/verification` — so there is nothing to extend there, and this task must not invent one. If web ever gains a shop sheet, it reads the same response.

**Two rules this must not break.**

A moderator is not a shop member. `resolveShopRole` returns `null` for them, so `activity.view` and `team.view` do not apply and must not be consulted — authorisation here is the moderator check the route already performs. Do not route a moderator through `requireShopPermission`; that would grant or refuse on the wrong axis.

The activity log's `metadata` carries before-and-after values, and `variant.cost_changed` carries cost figures. A moderator reviewing a shop has no business reading its margins. Strip `metadata` for `variant.cost_changed` entries in this response, and assert that in the test.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Create `packages/api/tests/int/moderation-shop-team.int.spec.ts`:

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { createFakePayload } from "./helpers/fakePayload";
import { GET } from "@/app/(frontend)/api/moderation/shops/[id]/route";
```

Assert, against a shop with an owner, an active manager, an active staff member, one revoked member and 25 activity entries of which one is `variant.cost_changed`:

- the response carries `team`: the three active members with `name`, `role` and `joinedAt`, and **not** the revoked one;
- no member object carries `inboxNotifications` or `revokedBy`;
- `activity` holds exactly 20 entries, newest first;
- the `variant.cost_changed` entry is present but its `metadata` is `null`;
- a caller who is neither admin nor moderator gets 403, and a shop member who is not a moderator also gets 403 — membership is not a moderation credential.

- [ ] **Step 2: Run it and watch it fail**

Run: `cd packages/api && bunx vitest run --config ./vitest.config.mts tests/int/moderation-shop-team.int.spec.ts`
Expected: FAIL — the response has no `team` or `activity` key.

- [ ] **Step 3: Extend the route**

In the existing moderator authorisation branch, after the shop is loaded, add the two reads and the cost-stripping. Keep them inside the same request, not a second round trip from the client.

- [ ] **Step 4: Run it and watch it pass**

Run the same command. Expected: PASS.

- [ ] **Step 5: Render it on mobile**

Create `ShopTeamCard.tsx` following `ShopHistoryList.tsx` for shape and `theme.ts` for tokens: the member rows with role chips and joined dates, then the activity list reusing the `shopActivity.actions.*` labels Task 3 added. Mount it in `ModerationScreen.tsx` beside `ShopHistoryList`. FR and EN in the same commit.

- [ ] **Step 6: Verify and commit**

```bash
cd packages/api && bun run test:int && bun run check-types
cd ../mobile && bun test && bunx tsc --noEmit | grep -c "error TS"
cd ../.. && bunx biome check --write packages/api/src/app/\(frontend\)/api/moderation/shops/[id]/route.ts packages/mobile/src/components/moderation/ShopTeamCard.tsx packages/mobile/src/components/moderation/ModerationScreen.tsx
git add packages/api/src/app/\(frontend\)/api/moderation/shops packages/api/tests/int/moderation-shop-team.int.spec.ts packages/mobile/src/components/moderation packages/mobile/src/locales
git commit -m "feat(moderation): show a shop's team and recent activity on the shop sheet"
```

Out of scope by the spec's own statement and deliberately absent: ownership transfer and multiple owners; custom roles and per-permission overrides; order permissions enforced in practice (P4); payment permissions in practice (P5); resale permissions in practice (P8); canned replies, response-time metrics and inbox SLAs (P9); the `conversations.unreadCount` mobile reads and the API never sets, unchanged; universal links for `/invite/{token}`; retention of order-linked conversations on account deletion (P4).

## Conflicts found while planning

Thirteen places where the spec contradicts itself, contradicts the code as it now stands, or would require something `AGENTS.md` forbids. Each carries a ruling and what it costs if the ruling is wrong.

1. **`requireShopPermission(req: PayloadRequest, shopId, permission)`.** No route in this repo holds a `PayloadRequest`; routes are shells over services that take `(payload, user: ServiceUser, shopId, options)`, and `requireShopMember`'s own comment already promises "callers keep passing the same options". **Ruling: the pure half (`ShopPermission`, `SHOP_PERMISSIONS`, `ROLE_PERMISSIONS`, `can`) lives in `access/shopRoles.ts` as the spec asks; `requireShopPermission(payload, user, shopId, permission, options?)` lives beside `requireShopMember` in `services/shopGuards.ts` (Task 1).** *Cost if wrong:* none functionally; the alternative is every one of ~20 call sites constructing a request object it does not otherwise need, plus a circular import between `access/shopRoles.ts` and `services/shopGuards.ts`.

2. **"`product-variants.cost` field read becomes `costs.view`, so staff no longer receive it."** It is already `shopRoleFieldAccess(canManageShop)` — owner and manager. `costs.view` has the same holders. **Ruling: the change is a rename; Task 2 says so in its own opening paragraph.** *Cost if wrong:* an implementer spends a round hunting a leak that does not exist, or "fixes" a field that was already correct.

3. **"Archiving a product needs `catalogue.archive`" — with no route named.** There is no archive route: archiving is `updateProduct` with `status: "archived"`. **Ruling: the check goes inside `updateProduct`, on the transition into `archived` only, so editing an already-archived product is not blocked (Task 2).** *Cost if wrong:* a manager cannot correct a typo on an archived product. Against: staff quietly removing products from the catalogue.

4. **"Shop closed (P1 `close`), in the close transaction" — pointing at `services/shops.ts`.** Closing lives in `services/shopListings.ts`'s `closeShopInTransaction`. **Ruling: the membership cascade goes there, before the `status: "closed"` write, because `resolveShopRole` returns `null` for every non-owner of a closed shop and the helper's own reads would then find nothing (Task 13).** *Cost if wrong:* a closed shop keeps active memberships that no longer grant anything, and the activity log never records why.

5. **The shared `@bns/chat-client` SDK the spec never mentions.** Both clients depend on it and neither declares a socket event of its own. **Ruling: `shop:inbox:join`, `shop:inbox:leave`, `inbox:conversation-updated` and `shop:access-revoked` are declared once, in `packages/chat-client-sdk/src/types.ts` (Task 15).** *Cost if wrong:* two divergent copies of an event payload, and the typed socket stops catching a renamed field — exactly what `AGENTS.md`'s "a type duplicated in two packages is a bug" forbids.

6. **"One `pending` invitation per `(shop, phone)` and per `(shop, email)`: partial unique index on `pendingKey`."** The spec gives the index but never says what releases the key. A revoked or declined invitation that kept its `pendingKey` would block that person forever. **Ruling: `pendingKey` is set to `null` on every terminal transition — revoke, accept, decline — and the partial filter is `{ status: "pending", pendingKey: { $type: "string" } }`, so a released row leaves the index entirely (Tasks 4, 9).** *Cost if wrong:* a shop can never re-invite someone who declined once, with no error that explains why.

7. **"`inboxNotifications` is readable only by the member and managers."** A field-level `access.read` receives the row and the request but has no cheap way to ask "is this row mine" — the row's `user` is a relation that may or may not be populated. **Ruling: the field uses `shopRoleFieldAccess((role) => can(role, "team.inviteStaff"))`, and `getShopTeam` adds the caller's own preference to their own row in the shaped view (Tasks 5, 9).** *Cost if wrong:* a staff member reading `shop-members` through Payload's REST does not see their own preference there; they see it on the team screen, which is where they change it.

8. **The `unread` inbox filter.** It depends on the caller's `conversation-reads` row, in a different collection, so it cannot be a Payload `Where` — and the spec also asks for a total per filter, which would be six count queries. **Ruling: `listShopInbox` reads the shop's conversations once with `pagination: false` and narrows in memory (Task 14).** *Cost if wrong:* a shop with thousands of conversations loads them all per request. Bounded by the shop's buyers, and the alternative is six queries plus a filter that cannot be expressed at all; P9's inbox metrics are the place to revisit it.

9. **Two members assigning the same conversation at once.** The spec is silent. **Ruling: `assignConversation` writes through `payload.db.updateOne` conditional on the assignee the caller decided against; the loser gets the winner's state back and exactly one `conversation.assigned` entry exists (Task 14, Review Focus 4).** *Cost if wrong:* a last-write-wins overwrite, two log entries for one change, and a member who believes they own a thread someone else is answering.

10. **`shop-team-paused` "in-app, push, email (owner)".** Novu decides channels per workflow, not per recipient; a per-recipient channel would need two workflows. **Ruling: one workflow declaring all three, with each subscriber's own preference governing (Task 12), and a comment on the entry saying so.** *Cost if wrong:* members receive an email about a pause as well as the owner. Against: two workflows whose copy has to be kept identical.

11. **`resolveShopRole` "keeps its signature and per-request cache."** It now needs the shop's status and level and the member's suspension, and it runs once per document through `shopRoleFieldAccess` on a public surface where `Users.read` is `anyone`. **Ruling: the signature is kept and two further per-request caches (`shopStateCache`, `userSuspensionCache`) are mandatory, with a test that asserts the second call reads nothing (Task 1).** *Cost if wrong:* P3 adds an N+1 against `shops` and `users` to every listing page — the shape of the mistake P2's `users.verified` ruling existed to avoid.

12. **"A pending invitation past this date is treated as expired at read time."** Nothing then ever writes `status: "expired"`, so the `expired` option exists and is unreachable. **Ruling: keep it. `isInvitationUsable` derives expiry everywhere, the public lookup reports `expired` without writing, and the enum value stays for a future job or a manual correction (Task 4).** *Cost if wrong:* an enum option no row ever holds. Against: a nightly job whose only purpose is to write a value every reader already derives.

13. **Account deletion: "they are excluded from the cascade's message deletion."** The cascade also deletes every conversation the user *participates in* — but a non-owner member is never a participant of a shop conversation, because `participants` is `[buyer, owner]`. So the conversations survive without a change, and only the messages need excluding. **Ruling: `reattributeShopMessages` runs first, re-attributes and returns the ids, and the existing `messageIds` query filters them out; the conversation query is untouched, and a test asserts the conversation survives (Task 13).** *Cost if wrong:* either a shop loses a whole thread when a staff member deletes their account, or a re-attributed message is deleted a moment later by the same cascade.

---

## Self-review

Run against the spec with fresh eyes after the plan was complete. What it found, and what was changed inline:

**1. Spec coverage.** Every section of the spec maps to a task in the table above. Three things the spec asks for had no task on the first pass and were added: the moderation shop sheet's Team section and last-20 entries (`recentShopActivity` in Task 3, exposed through the surfaces P1 and P2 already own), the `buildExpoPushData` cases the eight new workflows need for their deep links (Task 12), and the i18n parity gate the spec implies by "French and English kept in sync" but never asks for as a test (Task 30, in both clients).

**2. Placeholder scan.** Clean: no `TBD`, no "similar to Task N", no "add validation", no code step without code. Seven places carried a first draft plus a correction and were rewritten to their final form only: Task 5's `inboxNotifications` access, Task 9's `onCommitNotify`, Task 11's decline-route assertion, Task 13's `registerShopTeamLevelListener`, Task 14's `startConversation` create, Task 15's `inbox:conversation-updated` emit, Task 17's delete comment and Task 21's whole `invite-flow` block. The six mobile ports of a web module now carry the exact `cp` and `sed` commands instead of "the same file as web's".

**3. Type and name consistency across task boundaries.** Checked every `Produces` against every `Consumes`. Two mismatches were found and fixed: `notifyShopInboxMessage`'s stub in Tasks 7 and 9 lacked the optional `deps` parameter Task 12 implements, and `ServiceUser` needed an `email` field for `isChatServiceAccount` to work from a route (added in Task 14, which is the first task to need it). One ordering error was found and fixed: Tasks 4, 5 and 16 consume things Task 3 produces — `shopScopedRead`'s third parameter and `recordShopActivity` — so the wave table and the "cannot be parallelised" list now say `3 → 4, 5, 16`.

**4. Review Focus.** All five have a test pinned in the task that owns the code, each named in the task's own step: Task 15 (`evicts a removed user's rooms across nodes`, `message:send is refused after eviction`), Task 9 (`accept re-checks level, shop status and the seat count inside the transaction`; `a resend replaces the token and the old one stops working`), Task 14 (`two concurrent assignments produce one winner and one log entry`; `keeps a suspended assignee's assignment and flags it`) and Task 1 (`returns null for a suspended non-owner and keeps the owner's role`). Two further failure modes the spec implies got tests without a Review Focus line, because each is a single assertion rather than a class of input: the `pendingKey` E11000 race mapping to `team.invitationPending` (Task 9) and a non-base64url token answering 404 rather than throwing (Tasks 9, 11).
